melody sat 9 dB or more under; every song he agreed on had it within 8.
The reader's only loudness test is a quarter of the loudest raw voice
volume (12 dB, on volume, not on what renders), under a square root in
the melodic score, so a buried voice with a wide line wins.

**Built.** `bitcomposer.translate.lead`: a readings pass that renders each
tonal voice alone from its SPC, keeps every voice's loudness as a feature
(render/voice_lufs_<voice>), and relabels: the labelled melody keeps the
lead where it is heard (within LEAD_WINDOW, 9 dB, of the loudest tonal
voice), else the most varied heard voice (pitch-class entropy, then
loudness; not the bass, below the tune's ceiling) becomes the melody and
the old melody a counter-line, or an accompaniment where a counter
exists; the change is kept (lead/was_melody, now_melody) and the pass is
repeatable. The rule matched 12 of the owner's 14 picks (Locke: both
voices heard, he took the louder upper one; Gau: a loud low riff) and
relabels 9 FFVI songs of 66. `tools/salience.py` measures the margin on
any folder of whole songs with stems. Pack4's 94 tables refit on the
relabelled readings.

**Measured.** The pass relabelled 9 of 67 FFVI songs (Shadow, The
Phantom Forest, Gau, The Serpent Trench, Under Martial Law, Aria di
Mezzo Carattere, Epitaph, The Magic House, Umaro); FFVI's own labelled
tune's margin over the loudest other voice went from a median -3.2 to
-1.2 dB. The written songs did not follow: seeds 131-140 re-rendered on
the recounted pack sit a median 4.1 dB under (3.1 before), seeds 141-150
(Song Check 51) a median 7.2 under, two of ten on top. The reason, found
in Whole 149 (its token Kids Run Through the City, whose own tune sits
5.4 dB on top): the counted energy puts the tune on top, the render puts
the accompaniment there, and the accompaniment's second voice is the
cause. The token says the second voice plays at 3.2 times the line's
level; in the game that voice is another sample (a sustained one) with
a third of the line's notes, while the writer gives it the line's own
struck sample and most of the line's onsets. Over FFVI, a part's extra
voice is on another sample in 45 of 73 accompaniments, 27 of 42
counters and 15 of 15 basses, and plays 0.5-0.7 of the line's notes; the
written extra voices play 0.84-1.15 of the line's and take its sample in
the accompaniment half the time. The attack-to-body ratio of the two
samples (+3.4 dB on the accompaniment's, -3.1 on the tune's) adds the
rest. Next: the extra voices ride the token (each one's sample and its
note share over the line), and a note's loudness as it hears its sample
(strike, then body) in the level words.

### Round 65: the voices beside the line ride the token, and a note's loudness as it hears its sample (2026-10-29, from Whole 149)

**Found.** Round 64's relabel left the written tune where it was (seeds
131-140 a median 4.1 dB under the band, 3.1 before). Whole 149 showed
why: its token, Kids Run Through the City, has its tune 5.4 dB on top in
the game and the counted energy agrees, but the render puts the
accompaniment 3 dB over the tune. The token said the accompaniment's
second voice plays at 3.2 times the line's level; in the game that voice
is another sample, sustained, with a third of the line's notes (33 to
283), and the writer had given it the line's own struck sample and most
of the line's onsets. Over FFVI an extra voice is on another sample in
45 of 73 accompaniments, 27 of 42 counters and 15 of 15 basses, and
plays 0.5-0.7 of the line's notes; the written extra voices played
0.84-1.15 of the line's and took its sample in half the accompaniments.
The two samples' attack-to-body ratios (+3.4 dB on the accompaniment's,
-3.1 on the tune's) made the rest, and the level words counted and
played loudness by the loop body alone (round 56) while the estimate
already heard the strike (round 59).

**Built.** The balance token's sixth field names each voice beyond the
line as `level@sample@note share` (`rhythm.layer_tokens`); the plan keeps
the samples and shares (`layer_samples`, `layer_shares`); `samples_for`
gives the second voice the token song's own sample where the pack holds
it (the layer_samples pair else); the mask word `second_figures` is
counted and drawn under the voices' note share class (sparse under 0.4
of the line's notes, half under 0.8, full; `rhythm.LAYER_SHARES`,
`whole.layer_class`), its floor one deeper. A note's loudness is what it
hears of its sample, its strike for STRIKE_SECONDS then its body
(`rhythm.heard_rms`, shared), on both sides: the readings multiply a
note's level by it for the note's own length (`loudness_factor(key,
seconds)`, the strikes beside the loudness table), and the writer divides
the drawn level by the module sample's. Pack4's 94 tables refit.

**Measured** (tools/salience.py, K-weighted, the tune's margin over the
loudest other pitched line). Three states of the same ten seeds, 131-140:
Song Check 50 (round 63) median -3.1 dB, four tunes on top; the writer's
half of round 65 alone, before the tables were recounted (a crashed
refit left the old tables under the new code), median -2.6, four on top,
the best so far; the full round, tables recounted, median -4.6, two on
top. Seeds 151-160 (Song Check 52) on the full round: median -6.9, one on
top, Whole 156's tune 16 dB under its accompaniment. The chain is now
consistent end to end, and a consistent chain reproduces the game's own
balance, where the labelled tune sits a median 3.5 dB under; the half
state had leaned the tune forward of the game by counting the backing's
attacks on one side only. Whether the written tune should sit where the
game keeps it, or forward of it, is a question for the owner's ear, not
for the counts; Song Check 52 asks it.

### Round 66: the tune's grain, the voice's own alteration, and three songs' tokens (2026-10-29, from Song Check 52's notes: "the melody is still weak")

**Found.** The tune's structure is at parity with FFVI's (exact echoes
0.23 against 0.30, echoed bars 0.61 against 0.64, a hook 0.28 against
0.20); its grain is not. FFVI's medium tune has a median gap of half a
beat between onsets, 0.33-0.51 of them off the beat (quartiles over 39
songs) and 3.4-5.1 onsets a bar; four of Song Check 52's seven medium
tunes (152, 153, 156, 159) had a median gap of a whole beat, 0.15-0.21
off the beat, where five of FFVI's 39 sit, and 3.3-3.6 onsets a bar. The
bar table is conditioned on the bar before, so a tune's first draws fix
its grain, and the theme's echoes multiply them; a running-density hold
alone (the first half of this round) narrowed the density (medium 2.9-6.6
a bar, median 3.8) and left the grain where it was. The counter's
intervals drift where the tune's note is off the key's scale: FFVI's tune
is off it at 0.23 of the counter's onsets with it (its leading tones in
minor), and `scale_index` had put the counted steps back on the scale,
so a drawn unison with the tune's B# came out a B. Against a scale note
the written counter's intervals were "other" (seconds, tritones,
sevenths) in 0.12, FFVI's 0.12; against an off-scale note in 0.54,
FFVI's 0.15. The register fold changes no interval class (130 of 448
draws folded, all to the inversion). The three songs: Whole 151's token
is Dancing Mad (part 2), bass, counter and melody all at level 1.0 on
one organ sample over a snare-and-crash kit, and the plan followed it
to the letter; Whole 157's is Dancing Mad (part 1), its bass "main" in
three voices with the second at 1.9 times the line's level and 9.0 times
its notes (the line a sparse pedal); Whole 160's is New Continent, whose
kit is one crash at 0.5. A first reading of pack4's balance table as
stale was wrong: the tables count a note's level through the samples'
loudness (`rhythm.set_loudness` before the count), and with it set all
67 tokens match the readings. The token is drawn from all 67 songs; a
themed song may draw Dancing Mad's organ texture.

**Built.** A new counted word, `tune_grain` (`rhythm.tune_grain_units`,
`rhythm.GRAINS`): the share of a tune part's onsets off the beat as a
class (on-beat under 0.25, eighths to 0.5, fine over), one unit a song and
part (melody, counter), by the part and its habit's density tier (the
tier alone: under the whole habit the draw backed off to the part and
gave a dense tune "on-beat", which no dense tune of FFVI is). The plan
draws it on its own stream (`whole:{seed}:grain:{role}`) and the melody
writer holds its new rhythms' running share inside the class from the
first draw (`writer.grain_bounds`, GRAIN_SLACK 0.1), the density tier
after TIER_AFTER draws (`writer.tier_bounds`, TIER_SLACK 0.5). The
`voice_intervals` word is counted under the line note's state (`line`:
scale or altered) and, against a note off the scale, in semitones from the
note itself (`s-4`); `writer.interval_pitch` puts either back exactly and
`interval_weights` draws over the state's entries, weighing each by the
move its note would make from the voice's own last note (as `voice_moves`
counts it; the product had taken the move from the last note with the
line), in `beside_pitch` and the upper voices alike. A first form, a "+"
on a voice note off the scale, put alterations against scale notes they
never follow (the written "other" against scale notes rose from 0.12 to
0.22) and was dropped. Pack4 refit
(95 tables).

**Measured** (twenty seeds composed on the refit pack, 131-140 and
151-160, then the renders). The tune: medium tunes at 2.8-6.6 onsets a
bar, median 4.7 (FFVI's medium songs 3.4-5.1 by quartile, median 4.2),
their median gap half a beat in nine of ten (FFVI half a beat); the
off-beat share lands inside the drawn class in every tune but one (an
eighths tune at 0.68); 152 went from 0.14 off the beat to 0.40, 153 from
0.15 to 0.35. Seeds 161-170: medium tunes 4.3-5.8 a bar, all at a
half-beat gap but one. The counter, with the tune's onset: "other"
intervals 0.11 of its notes (FFVI 0.12; before 0.19); against a tune
note off the scale 0.15 (FFVI 0.15; before 0.54); alone, 0.31 (FFVI
0.29). The balance (tools/salience.py, K-weighted): seeds 131-140 a
median 3.7 dB under the loudest other pitched line, three on top (round
65: 4.6 under, two; round 63: 3.1, four); 161-170 a median 4.2 under, two
on top (Song Check 52's 151-160: 6.9, one). Song Check 53 (seeds 161-170)
asks the ear whether the melody now moves like the game's and whether the
cadences' sour counter is gone; the token draw's reach (Dancing Mad's
organ among the themed songs) is put to the owner as a question, not
built.

### Round 67: the sustain loop reaches the render, the bridge read again (2026-10-30, from the owner's listening on Ragnarok: "the melody is just a little blip behind the music")

**Found.** Every written render since the score bridge (5284664,
2026-10-21) had gone out with no looped sample. A preset voice carries its
loop as the module's sustain loop, held while the key is down and released
into the tail after it, and `score._Pcm` read the plain loop flag alone, so
the SNES back end took every sustaining voice as a one-shot. On the built-in
sets that is a 48 ms square under the tune and pads that pluck; on an
imported pack's registered samples (FFVI's run 125 ms to a second) the
parts decayed like plucks, which read as weak levels rather than as a blip,
and Song Checks 40 to 53 were all heard that way, while the FFVI reference
renders (`brr.decode` keeps the BRR loop flag) loop every sample. The
writer's level math (`sample_strike_and_body`) reads both loops, so it set
levels for sounds that never sustained. Measured on the owner's three songs
on bitComposer's own sounds (stems by muting channels, through libopenmpt,
the player's engine): the tune 9.7 to 21.6 LU under the loudest other
pitched line; with the loop carried 2.1 to 6.6 under, and 5.1 over in one,
the tune up 11 to 18 dB. Beside it: a drum voice's levels the import could
not read are NaN, the readings' median over them was NaN, three of the 67
balance tokens carried "nan" for the drums, and a written song that drew
one played its kit at full (`min(1.0, nan)` is 1.0: Hidden Frontier's and
Causeway of Lanterns' crash at 64).

**Fixed.** `_Pcm(sample, sustain=True)` from the writer's instrument table
takes the sustain loop as the chip's loop (Classic's songs keep their
one-shots, the owner's call); the readings skip the unread drum levels, the
balance token never carries a non-finite level (the plan's `levels` draw
stands in), and the plan ignores a "nan" a shipped table still holds; a
test for each. The render bridge: with the loops carried, Song Check 53's
and 50c's twenty seeds rendered a median 4.3 and 4.1 dB over their targets
(quartiles +3.7..+4.7 and +3.6..+4.3), so `RENDER_BRIDGE` is -17.4 from
-21.7; re-rendered, the residual is +0.3 (quartiles -0.4..+2.0) and -0.1
(-0.4..+0.7).

**Measured, the same seeds as Song Check 53 (161-170) and 50c (131-140),
LUFS.** Tune margin over the loudest other pitched line: 161-170 median
-6.9 (quartiles -7.7..-2.2; one of ten on top; Song Check 53 as heard:
-4.2, two on top), 131-140 median -1.7 (four of ten on top). Each part under
the whole, median, against FFVI's own renders (ffvi-stems, 63 songs): bass
-4.0 (FFVI -7.0), accompaniment -4.0 (-6.3), counter -6.4 (-4.1), melody
-7.8 (-7.2), drums -21.3 (-9.5); 131-140: bass -3.8, melody -5.6, counter
-8.6, accompaniment -10.9, drums -18.6. So the tune sits where FFVI's does;
the bass runs 3 dB forward of FFVI's place, the counter 2 dB behind it, and
the drums 11 dB behind. The one-shot renders had hidden the bass's and the
accompaniment's sustain (the tune's samples are struck ones more often),
which is why the measured tune margin fell as the loops came back. Whole 170
shows the voice count outweighing the level: the token's loudest part is
the tune at 1.0, and it renders 7 dB under a three-voice accompaniment and a
three-voice counter at 0.9.

**Open, for the rounds after the owner's ear on Song Check 54.** The kit's
level: the token's drums level (0.1 to 0.3 of the top in most tokens)
renders the kit 11 dB under FFVI's place, so the counting of the kit's level
against the pitched parts is off by about that much, where round 63's strike
rule had closed 7 dB on one-shot renders. The bass 3 dB forward and the
counter 2 dB behind, both from the token's counted levels: a counting
question (the sides' mean, the loudness factor at the played register)
before any writer rule. Voices beside the line add up: a part's level is one
voice's and its second and third voices add to it, and FFVI's token songs
had the same voices without coming out that way, so the layer shares want a
look. Every measurement of rounds 56 to 66 that set written renders against
FFVI's (the loudness tracking, round 59's stems report, the salience of
rounds 64 to 66) was made on one-shot renders and stands to be read again.

### Round 68: the kit's level a piece (2026-10-30, from Song Check 54's measurement: the kit 9 to 16 dB under its token's song)

**Found.** Whole 169's balance token is Blackjack's. Blackjack's own render
(the readings' per-part renders, ffvi-stems) plays its kick and snare voice
at volume column 64 and its hat voice at 24; Whole 169 played its kick at 6
and its hat at 28, its drums -25.6 LUFS under the whole against Blackjack's
-9.4, and over Song Check 53's ten seeds the kit sat a median -21.3 under
the whole against FFVI's -9.5. The cause was in the count, not the writer.
A song's level for a part (`rhythm.load`) is one number, the median over
every note of every voice of the part, each note's level times its sample's
loudness factor (the strike's RMS for a drum, round 63); for the drums the
hat is the most frequent piece and its strike the quietest, so the median
is the hat's, and the token carried `drums=::::0.2` where the kick plays
near full. On the other side, `render.level_of` played every drum note at
that one level over its own sample's strike, bringing every piece to the
same heard loudness: the kick as quiet as a hat, the hat relatively loud,
the kit's structure inverted against the source. Counted a piece over
Blackjack's drum voices: the kick's notes at a median level 0.33 raw and
0.53 heard (its strike 0.40, factor 1.62), the snare's 0.33 raw and 0.22
heard, the hat's 0.125 raw and 0.056 heard; as shares of the loudest part
(the bass, 0.31): kick 1.7, snare 0.7, hat 0.2, where the part's one
median gave 0.2. Over FFVI's 46 kits with a level: the kick sits at a
median 1.6 of the loudest pitched part (quartiles 1.2-2.2; 15 of 17 over
1.0), the snare at 0.3 (0.2-0.6), the hat at 0.2 (0.1-0.2), the tom at
1.0, the crash at 0.6 (0.5-0.8), the rim at 0.9 (0.4-1.2); the loudest
piece is twice the part's one level or more in 22 of the 46.

**Built.** The kit's level a piece, as a counted unit on the token. The
readings keep each drum sample's median level (`Song.kit_levels`, the
same loudness logic as the part's level, the NaN guard of round 67 kept);
the balance token's kit field carries it beside each piece's sample as a
share of the song's loudest part's level in tenths, as the part level is
(`kit_token(s, top)`:
`K=sbfe716b9b6@1.7,S=s1279e119a1@0.7,h=sd1c4c7ce33@0.2`; a piece whose
level the readings never heard keeps the old form `K=sid`, and a token
with the old form still loads); the plan parses it into
`extras.drums.kit_levels` (the log line "kit from the balance: KSh (K
sbfe716b9b6 at 1.7, ...)"; `plan.kit_piece_level` by kit slot, the open
hat at the hat's) and `render.note_level` plays a drum note on a kit slot
at its piece's share over the top level, the part's share (the fifth

### Round 70: the token's own voices, the echo as counted, the apart voice apart (2026-10-30, from round 69)

**Found.** Beyond round 69's countings: the one `:second` sample and bound
served every voice beside a line, so Good Night's four extra voices all took
the first's sample (the doubling voice's) and its home band, which was the
line's own band (56-64), and an "apart +8" voice was folded into it; a pedal
line (Overture plays its accompaniment on a snare sample at one pitch, which
is how the game plays it) never shared its note with the voices beside it,
so 167's apart voice found nothing to take its interval from. And the
strike window was off for tonal samples too, the other way: the instrument
builder writes a release tail after a pack voice's loop, and the strike read
over the module's data ran into it (7 dB under the readings' on a 32-frame
string loop).

**Built.** (1) The token's fifth field is the LINE voice's median level
(`readings.part_levels`: the line as the layer words see it; the pooled
median for a one-voice part, the drums, or a line never read) and the
eighth the line voice's sample, so line times each sixth-field share
reproduces every voice; `note_level` plays a voice beside the line at the
token's share for that voice, doubling or not (the line's level only where
the token has none), and `samples_for` registers each voice's own token
sample (`role:v`, `sample_of` reads it first). Troops' token now reads
`counter=...:0.4:2.5@sfd6accf98a...` with `bass=...:0.9:1.6@s1d296a2572@1.93
::sa692eac0a6` and the melody the loudest. (2) The kit's piece level in
hundredths (`K=sbfe716b9b6@1.70`; tenths still read); a pack voice's sample
carries the recording's own rate and frames, and the strike is read over
the recording's first 150 ms by them: the kit's 11 samples at 0.00 dB from
the readings' strike, the tonal samples' strike-to-body ratio within 0.001
dB of the WAV's. (3) The `echo` word: a reading keeps the chip's echo at
key-on (`reading.echo_of_notes`, features of source `echo`; FFVI's 82
added from the SPCs by round 70's `tools/echo_features.py`, since a re-read
would have lost the lead pass's relabels), counted one unit a song as
`0.20:80:0.78:accompaniment+counter+melody:bass+drums` (67 units; the tune
sending in 66 of 66, the counter 51 of 53, the accompaniment 47 of 60, the
bass 36 of 63, the drums 9 of 47), drawn once on `whole:SEED:echo` by the
writer bare in place of `ECHO_WET` and the delay names, the sound set's
habits standing without the word; the player's echo path untouched. (4) An
apart or alternating voice of an upper part in its own span
(`render.voice_bounds`: the line's written span at the word's offset,
within the sample's whole played range); a pedal line shares its note.
Pack4 refit (96 tables) and the shipped vocabulary written again from it.
Tests for each; the suite at 389.

**Measured, seeds 161-170 on the refit pack, LUFS** (`~/diags/bitcomposer/
fable/round70/`: `q1.txt`, `q3_overlap.txt`, `q3_echo.txt`,

(-4.1; -6.3), counter -5.8 (-6.5; -4.1), melody -6.6 (-8.1; -7.2), drums

(Troops): the counter line at 0.4 of the top with its second at 2.5 on

by the old `:second` rule and wants the `role:v` keys. The apart voice's

voice_moves) are folded into the span rather than conditioned on the
offset; a voice whose sample's whole range cannot hold the offset keeps the
sample's wall. The bass's and the counter's second voices keep their old

### Round 71: measured, the note's envelope in the counted level; the pack's envelope reaches the written note (2026-10-30, from round 70's open item on the basses)

**Measured first, nothing built on it**
(`~/diags/bitcomposer/fable/round71/measure.md`, its scripts in `tools/`
beside it). The question: the bass 2 to 4 dB forward of its token song's on
163, 169 and 170 with a count that matches, and the readings' level blind
to the envelope the game plays a note with. (1) The envelope the game plays
the token songs' bass and melody voices with (161 Setzer, 163 Troops March
on, 164 Johnny C. Bad, 167 Overture 3, 169 Blackjack, 170 The Prelude; the
SPC decoded as the import decodes it, ADSR1/ADSR2 or GAIN at key-on per
note): every one of the fifteen voices plays all its notes with ONE
envelope, ADSR with attack 15 and decay 7 to sustain level 7, the sustain
rate alone differing by sample (0 holds, 12 to 16 decay), no GAIN mode, no
rewrite while sounding; and in every case it is the envelope the pack
stores for that sample. Over all of FFVI's notes on pack samples, 51 of the
67 samples play one envelope 95 % of the time or more, the kit's eleven and
every bass and lead sample above 98 to 100 %; sixteen pads and sustained
voices vary their sustain rate by song, none of them a bass. FFVI's
envelope is a property of the sample, which the pack already carries. (2)
The envelope's effect on heard loudness, simulated with the chip's envelope
(`snes_chip.envelope_track`) over the looped pack WAV for the voice's
median note length, RMS of envelope times sample over the sample's: the
game's bass notes lose 0.0 dB on sa692eac0a6 (163, 167), 0.3 on s014dbedd79
(164), 1.2 on s1d296a2572 (169), 8.1 on s8320f1bea2 over The Prelude's
1.57 s notes (170); the pack's envelope over the written voice's note
length the same within 0.2 dB except 170's (2.2 over 0.29 s notes). The
predicted gap (ours minus theirs) against the measured: 163's bass 0.0
against +2.5 (r70), 169's +0.1 against +3.9, 170's +5.9 against +2.4; over
the twelve bass and melody parts r = −0.05 with round 69's per-voice
residual gaps and +0.24 with r70's part gaps. None of the three basses
within 2 dB, so the per-part envelope on the token was not built. (3) The
readings' `loudness_factor` and the writer's `heard_rms` make the same
assumption, neither seeing the envelope, and no FFVI attack is slower than
2 chip samples; the two sides measure alike.

**Found instead.** Whole 169's bass instrument in the r70 module carries a
two-node volume envelope, flat at 64, where Blackjack's bass instrument on
the same sample carries six nodes, the converter's rendering of ADSR FF/EE;
the notes `score_of` composed for 169 carried ADSR (0, 0) and GAIN 127
(direct at full: flat) on every part. `samples_for`'s `make` names a
pack's own sample by its bare id (`s1d296a2572`) while the pack registers
the voice as `ffvi_spc_s1d296a2572`, and `put` looked the envelope up by
the bare name alone. Both date from the envelope's own commit (38f98e1,
2026-10-24; `make` from the whole writer's first, 75ab8dd), so no written
render on an imported pack had ever played the pack's envelope: every
note went out flat at full gain, and the decaying samples (the basses on
s1d296a2572 at SR14 and s014dbedd79 at SR12, The Prelude's s8320f1bea2 at
SR16, s4fd4b0992b, s72f0a1e88e) sustained where the game lets them fall.
With ours flat the envelope predicts +1.2 of 169's +3.9, +0.3 of 164's
+0.4, +1.3 of 170's melody's +1.2, and none of 163's (a flat sample on
both sides). Also ruled out for 169: the voices' pans and sides (centred
on both sides, nothing inverted), the module's channel, instrument and
sample volumes (equal in every pair). The Prelude's voices swell (32 of
its 34 bass notes change volume while sounding; the readings' level is the
key-on volume), a count 170 does not see.

**Fixed.** `render.sample_envelope` reads the pack's envelope under the
voice's registered name as well as the bare id; a test that a written note
on a pack voice carries the pack's ADSR or GAIN and a sample without one
stays flat and full. The pack and the readings are unchanged (no refit;
the shipped vocabulary stands). The suite at 390.

**Measured, seeds 161-170 on the fixed writer, LUFS**
(`sustain/r71`, stems and `salience.txt`; `round71/m4.txt`). The same
songs as r70 (form, band, tempo, columns and global volumes unchanged);
Whole 169 now carries a decaying envelope on 10 of its 34 instruments (0
before), 170 on 8 of 8, 163 on 1 of 15. Each part under the whole, median,
r71 (r70; FFVI's 63): bass -4.2 (-4.2; -7.0), accompaniment -4.3 (-4.3;
-6.3), counter -4.8 (-5.8; -4.0), melody -7.0 (-6.6; -7.2), drums -14.4
(-13.5; -9.4); the tune's margin -4.2 (-3.3; FFVI -3.5), two of ten on
top. The three basses against their token songs, r71 (r70): 163 +2.5
(+2.5), 169 +3.1 (+3.9), 170 +1.4 (+2.4); 164's +1.1 (+0.4), 161's +1.2,
167's +1.3. Unchanged on 161, 162, 165 and 167 (flat samples throughout);
164's counter -3.5 (s4fd4b0992b at SR16 under short notes), 168's
accompaniment -2.3, 170's accompaniment +2.9 relative to a whole that
dropped with its long bass and tune notes.

**Open.** 169's bass still +3.1 over Blackjack's with the sample, level,
column times global volume, envelope, pan and register all the same, so
the rest is not in the count of a note's level: the duty runs the other
way (Blackjack's bass sounds 0.90 of the time, ours 0.60), the salience
RMS is gated and the LUFS relative to a whole that is itself 1.5 dB
quieter than Blackjack's on the same target, which alone puts every part
of ours 1.5 dB more forward. A voice's swells (The Prelude) have no word.
Every measurement of rounds 60 to 70 that set written renders against
FFVI's was made with flat envelopes on the written side, as round 67's
were with one-shots; the decaying samples' parts (164's counter, 168's

### Round 72: the default player's sound, after the writer (2026-10-30, from the owner's listening on the `bit` set before round 67 and round 71's envelope finding)

The writer (`translate/`) is untouched this round; the bare path is held
byte for byte. What GENERATE plays on bitComposer's own sounds through
`performance.py` was three rules and a flat voice, and is now two counted
words and a shaped one.

**Found.** (1) The default player's echo was its own rule (a delay by tempo
and density, a wetness name over `score.ECHO_WET` 0.4): 0.24 to 0.6 of full
where round 69 counted FFVI at 0.20 in every song, and dry in a share of
songs where FFVI's word is never dry. (2) Vibrato was a fixed rule (10 cents
at 5.5 Hz after 0.12 s on every note of the tune and the counter over
0.35 s; Hidden Frontier had 18 such notes), uncounted. The readings keep no
pitch writes (six fields a note), so FFVI's 82 SPCs were decoded again as
the import decodes them and each note's pitch register while it sounds read
as vibrato where it swings back and forth through 1.5 cycles or more of 3
cents or more (a slide is monotonic and counts as nothing, a swing of 90
cents or more is a trill, counted apart; `~/diags/bitcomposer/fable/round72/
measure.md`, `vibrato_summary.txt`). FFVI, 143,679 pitched notes: the tune's
4,763 held notes carry vibrato in 926 (0.19), the counter's in 0.10, the
accompaniment's 0.08, the bass's 0.07, the drums' 0.02; 0.69 of the tune's
held notes carry no bend at all. Depth (cents either side) on the tune 16.4
/ 29.9 / 45.2 at the quartiles, the counter 12.7 / 18.8 / 43.4; the rate 5.7
Hz in most songs (the driver's usual) and 15 to 19 Hz in about a third of
the songs with any (Blackjack 15.5, Celes 14.7, Battle Theme 18.6, Another
World of Beasts 17.2); the onset a median 0.5 s from key-on (quartiles 0.4
to 0.8), so a note held 0.35 to 0.5 s carries it in 0.01 of cases, 0.5 to 1
s in 0.10, 1 to 2 s in 0.29, 2 s and over in 0.30, and 11 of 29,079 notes
under 0.35 s carry any. The first swing is 0.41 of the note's depth at the
median (under half in 0.56 of 2,706 notes): the driver ramps the depth in.
By song, 33 of the 67 tunes in the vocabulary carry none at all and the
rest run from 0.03 to 0.91 of their held notes (The Empire Gestahl 39 of
43, Terra 51 of 85): a per-song habit before a per-note one. (3) The `bit`
voices carried no envelope: every note flat at full gain, then a hard cut,
where round 71 found FFVI plays every sample with attack 15, decay 7 to
sustain level 7, then rate 0 (hold) or 12 to 16 (fall). (4) `bit`'s lead
pool listed the square twice.

**Built.** (1) `performance.echo_for` draws the `echo` word by the writer's
own draw (`render.echo_word`) on the performance's stream
(`written:SEED:performance:echo`), so the two paths cannot drift; the
sound set's habits (`render.echo_of`, as for the writer bare) stand where a
vocabulary has no echo word, and `describe` says which ("echo 80 ms at
0.20 of full (the vocabulary's word)"). `--echo off` holds for a written
song; the other echo flags stay Classic's. (2) The `vibrato` word: a
note's vibrato read at import (`reading.note_vibrato`), each voice's held
notes counted by length class (`reading.vibrato_of_notes`, features of
source `vibrato`, written by `store.keep`; FFVI's 82 backfilled by
`tools/vibrato_features.py` after `readings.db` was copied to `.r72`), one
unit a part a song (`rhythm.vibrato_units`: the share of the part's held
notes carrying it in each of the four classes in tenths, "-" for a class
the song held no note in, then the depth to 5 cents, the rate to 0.5 Hz,
the onset to 0.1 s; `0.0/0.0/1.0/0.0:20:12.5:0.6`). The player draws it a
part for the tune and the counter on `written:SEED:performance:vibrato`
(`performance.vibrato_words`) and each held note takes it with its class's
share at the word's depth and rate from its onset, rising over one cycle;
without the word the old constants stand and the message says so. The
parts are still the tune's and the counter's; the other parts' words are
counted and not played. Pack4 refit (`pack.json.r72` the backup; 97
tables, 250 vibrato units of 67 songs) and the shipped vocabulary written
again from it (`tools/ship_default.py`). (3) Every pitched `bit` voice is
registered with an SNES ADSR (`packs/bit.py ENVELOPES` through
`samples.register_presets` into `SAMPLE_ENVELOPES`, the place a pack's
sample keeps its game's; `render.put` plays it with no new code path):
attack 15, decay 7 to level 7, sustain rate 0 (ADSR 0xFF 0xE0) for every
one. The sustaining voices because they hold: square, pulse, saw lead,
sine flute, ocarina, brass, horn, reed, synth voice, violin, cello,
strings, low strings, warm pad, glass pad, choir, organ, brass pad, wind
choir, fretless, bowed bass. The struck and plucked voices because their
decay is in their data (the `_struck` recipe shapes the whole sample under
an exponential and freezes its last two periods at the decayed level; a
falling rate would double it): piano (its frozen tail at 0.22 of the
strike), e-piano (0.26), bell (0.21), music box (0.135), pluck (0.10),
harp (0.135), nylon (0.135), marimba (0.016), kalimba (0.05), sub bass
(0.26), picked bass (0.17), synth bass (0.20), slap bass (0.10); so a held
struck note keeps sounding at its tail as it did. The kit's one-shots carry
none. Timbres untouched. (4) The lead pool lists the square once (the
set's own list, not a game's count). Tests for each; the suite at 395 (one skipped).

**Measured, seeds 68554, 87326, 305253 and 1-10 through the default
player's path on `bit`, LUFS, before (items 1 and 2 in, no envelope, the
old pool) and after** (`round72/bit-before`, `bit-after`, `salience.txt` in
each). Each part under the whole, median, before -> after: bass -3.5 ->
-3.6, accompaniment -5.9 -> -5.9, counter -7.3 -> -7.3, melody -7.8 ->
-7.8, harmony -10.2 -> -10.3, drums -18.2 -> -18.2; the tune's margin over
the loudest other pitched line -6.5 -> -6.5 (quartiles -8.7..-0.8 ->
-8.7..+0.5), three of thirteen on top both times. Ten of the thirteen songs
moved no part by more than 0.1 dB; the three that moved changed their
tune's voice through the pool's shortened list (Caldera of Glass square ->
pulse, melody -4.7 -> -4.4; The Cloister of Silk ocarina -> brass, melody
-5.5 -> -3.5 and counter -3.7 -> -5.4, its margin -1.8 -> +1.9; The Endless
Arcade sine flute -> brass, melody -14.4 -> -13.5), not through the
envelope. So the envelope's own effect on the balance is under 0.1 dB
(a decay to 7/8 and a hold lowers every pitched part alike), where the loop
fix moved parts 11 to 18 dB. The echo the thirteen carry: 0.20 of full at
80 ms in every one, feedback 0.55 or 0.78, the sends the word's song's.
Vibrato under the word on the ten owner seeds: 0, 0, 43, 0, 15, 4, 22, 8,
14, 5 notes (68554 Hidden Frontier's tune draws a word with none, the
game's most common case; 305253's 20 cents at 12.5 Hz on the tune and 5.5
Hz on the counter from 0.6 to 0.8 s in).

**For the owner's ear** (`~/diags/bitcomposer/idiom/phase4/whole/sustain/
r72-bit/`): seeds 68554, 87326, 305253 and 1-7 as GENERATE writes them, by
title with a `.plan.json` each (title, seed, key, bpm, form, band, the echo

-8.4 (-7.2), drums -17.7 (-9.5); the tune's margin -6.4 (quartiles
-8.1..+2.3; FFVI -3.5, -7.0..+0.3), three of ten on top (FFVI 18 of 63).
On the `bit` set the bass runs 2 dB forward of FFVI's place, the counter 2.5
behind, the tune 1 behind, and the kit 8 dB behind: the kit's levels are

flute and brass loop one period with no motion) and the "tune on top"
question are the owner's; not built. The vibrato word's parts beyond the
tune and the counter (counted, not played); the per-note draw spreads a
part's share evenly where the game clusters it by passage; the "-" classes
fragment the word's outcomes (one unit a song). The struck `bit` voices'
frozen tails hold at 11 to 36 dB under their strike for as long as a note
is held, where FFVI lets its decaying samples fall at rate 12 to 16: the
owner's call to hear before a falling rate is given. The `bit` kit 8 dB
under FFVI's place. The vocabulary map cannot name the performance as a
draw site (it reads the writer's sources), so `vibrato` shows as drawn by
no writer, which is true.

### Round 73: the part's attack class counted, and a built-in set's pool rule matches it (2026-10-31, from Song Checks 59 and 60: "the melody is sitting behind again")

**Found.** With the bit set's range tops placed by the owner's ear (the
bright single-period leads capped at C5 to F#5), the pool rule gave six of
Song Check 60's ten default tunes to the keys, the e-piano, piano, pluck
and nylon, which range 36 to 96 and, being struck, take any speed, where
the sustaining leads stop at 4 to 8 notes a second. A struck voice decays
14 to 20 dB after its attack, so a tune on one fades between its notes
and reads weak at any measured level: Northern Tidepool's e-piano measured
the loudest part of its song and the owner heard it hiding. FFVI's tunes
ride a struck sample in 3 of 66 songs (Awakening, Spinach Rag, Good
Night; the median strike over body is -1.7 dB), its basses in 6 of 63, its
accompaniments in 5 of 60. Over the twenty default-set verdicts of Song
Checks 59 and 60 the ear otherwise followed the measured margin: forward
or fine at -7 LU and better, buried at -9.3 and under whatever the voice,
the horn at -10.3 included, so the dark-voice (ear-weighting) idea of
round 69's follow-up is not needed.

**Built.** The `attacks` word (`rhythm.attack_units`, KEPT, shape part):
whether the sample a part's line plays most is struck, its first 150 ms
more than twice (6 dB) the RMS of its loop body, or sustaining, one unit a
part a song, with the samples' loudness as `set_loudness` holds it.
`packs.profile` carries `struck` for a built-in voice; `render.attack_for`
draws the class on `whole:{seed}:attack:{role}` for a part the pool rule
chooses a voice for, and `render.best_sample(attack=)` lets a built-in
voice of the other class stand down wherever one of the class is there
to choose; a pack's own samples carry no profile and are not sorted by
it, and an imported pack's parts take the token's sample before the pool
rule anyway. Pack4 refit (98 tables; `attacks` 208 units of 67 songs:
melody 63 sustaining and 3 struck, bass 57 and 6, accompaniment 55 and 5,
harmony 15 and 0, arp 4 and 0) and the shipped vocabulary written again
from it. Tests: the unit's class from a stub's loudness, nothing without
it; the pool rule's pick by class on two bit voices.

**Measured, Song Check 61 (five songs on FFVI's sounds, seeds 171-175,
and five from the default composer, seeds 18-22, all through the
player's performance).** All five default tunes drew sustaining and got
the horn, ocarina, cello, sine flute and violin; none a key. Tune margin
over the loudest other pitched line (LUFS): FFVI sounds +5.5, +3.6, -0.7,
+3.2, -5.4 (median +3.2, three of five on top); default +11.2, +2.1, -5.5,
-6.6, -8.0 (median -5.5, two on top). The two margins under on the default
set are the tokens' (the tune at 0.3 and 0.5 of a bass at 1.0); the +11.2
is a token with the tune at 1.0 over everything else at 0.2 to 0.4.
Default melodies' centroids 226 to 837 Hz (the horn's 226 at a low
register, the violin's 816), none above 2 % of energy over 2 kHz except
the violin's 7 %; the FFVI tunes 761 to 1365 Hz.

**Open.** The default player's deep margins are now the token's balance,
FFVI's own habit (the tune under another line in 71 % of its songs); a
"tune on top" choice in the performance layer stays the owner's. The
bass and pad voices of the bit set hold where FFVI's basses decay (round
72's envelope choice): the bit bass sits 2 to 4 dB forward of FFVI's
place. Beacon of Lanterns' second bass voice runs sixteenths at sixteen a
second: the bass figure tables against FFVI's second bass voices, a count
to make. The harmony drew a lead-pool voice through the wider reach in
Dance of the Harbor: the reach's own class should follow the word too.

### Round 74: a kit piece the balance's song struck on a tonal sample (2026-10-31, from Song Check 61's Crossing of Steel: "a repetitive noise that starts at about 10 seconds and goes on for the whole song")

**Found.** Not the echo or the vibrato: the second drums voice striking the
counter's own synth lead sample (sf89f5aedfd) at the kit's one note, G#2,
thirteen times a bar from bar 4 to the end. The song's token is The
Phantom Forest, whose kit is one piece, `o` on that lead sample at 0.38 of
the top: in that song a pitched voice plays the lead seven times in 95

reader labelled it drums, the kit token carried it, and round 63's

bass sample sa692eac0a6 as a tom in three songs, leads and pads as `o` or
`c` in the rest; some FFVI really strikes, some misreads like this one. And
the figure: with the habit "sparse repeating" the drums table gave the lone
`o` kit 13 hits a bar, because the kit-conditioned context has two songs
(one a shaker at 13.5 hits a bar) and the draw's back-off drops the habit
before the kit; FFVI's own lone-`o` kits play 0.1, 1.5 and 13.5 hits a bar.

**Built.** `samples_for` takes a token kit sample into a writer's slot only
where the pack classes it as percussion (`samples.is_percussion`, the drum
namer's class at import); a tonal one leaves the pack's own drum in the
slot and the plan logs "kit pieces the balance's song struck on tonal
samples, the pack's own drums standing in". A test with a stubbed pack.

**Measured, Crossing of Steel (seed 173 on FFVI's sounds, the player's
performance), before and after.** The kit's slot plays the pack's rim
instead of the lead at G#2; hits unchanged at 13 a bar; drums -12.3 under
the whole (from -14.2), every other part within 0.1 dB. Melody, bass and
accompaniment decode sample for sample identical. The counter does not:
same notes, sample and envelope, but before the fix the kit had registered
the lead sample first and the counter shared its slot at the kit's tuning,
C5 speed 32834 against the counter's own 32825, half a cent; retuning the
after stem by that ratio brings the two to 0.98 correlation. Song Check 62
holds the two renders side by side.

**Open.** The drums figure's density for a lone piece: the table's back-off
order (part, meter, kit, tempo, habit, feel, previous) drops the habit
before the kit, so a kit with few songs behind it inherits another song's
density; FFVI's lone-piece kits are mostly sparse. A back-off that keeps
the habit longer is a change to every drums draw and wants its own round
with the FFVI measures beside it. The drums role reader: a pitched voice
with a handful of notes a song is an accent, not a kit; a check against
the phase 1 ground truth before any reader change. The kit token could
carry the pitch each piece was struck at, as it carries the level. And

### Round 75: the kit piece's struck note, the kit at its habit's density, the tempo inside its class (2026-10-31, from Song Check 62: "a snare is super out of place on this song")

**Why round 74 came out.** Round 74's rule, a tonal kit piece leaves the
pack's own drum in its slot, was a writer rule standing where a count
should be: FFVI's pack has no "other"-class percussion (15 drum samples:
6 snares, 5 hats, 2 cymbals, a kick, a tom), the import fills the rim slot
with the snare, so every tonal "o" piece became a snare, and of FFVI's 47
kit tokens 11 carry an "o" piece and 10 of those are tonal samples (a lead
struck as an accent, bells, pads). Five tokens have nothing but tonal
pieces. The owner's question, whether the kit knows the song it is
chosen for: it does, through one door, the balance token, which is the
song's own kit; what did not know the song was the slot's stand-in and
the figure's density.

**Built.**

1. The kit token carries the note each piece is struck at most, after its
   level (`o=sf89f5aedfd@0.38@60`, `c=sid@@79` without a level;
   `Song.kit_pitches`, the most struck rounded note, the lower on a tie).
   Of the 101 pieces in FFVI's 47 kit tokens, 37 carry a note (18 snares,
   10 "o", 6 toms, 2 kicks, a cymbal); the one tonal "o" without one is
   Overture (part 2)'s, whose notes the reading left unpitched.
2. Every kit sample of the token's song the pack holds takes its slot
   again (round 63). `render.kit_note_of`: a percussion sample strikes at
   KIT_NOTE as before (registered to sound as recorded there); a tonal one
   at the token's note, or where none at the note that plays the
   recording at its own rate. The Phantom Forest's lead at C4, where the
   game plays it, not at the kit's note two octaves under.
3. The kit at its habit's density. The drums table conditions on the kit
   before the habit (round 58), so a kit with no song behind it in this
   meter and tempo class took another song's bars at that song's
   density: The Phantom Forest's one-piece kit in 3/4 wrote Johnny C. Bad's
   13 snare hits a bar in 4/4 under "sparse repeating", The Wedding
   Waltz's 3/4 snare (4 a bar) wrote Opening Theme's 13.
   `writer.kit_table_reaches`: the bars the kit table would draw for this
   kit, hits a bar over every bar with the rests as `_rhythm_habit` counts
   a song's tier, must sit under the tier's top (DENSITIES: sparse 2,
   medium 6, dense open); where they do not, the plan writes the kit from
   its limbs' tables (drums-KSt, drums-h, drums-c, drums-o) at their own
   counted group habits, the path a pack without a kit table already
   took. The never-silent redraw (round 58) and the last resort keep to
   the group's habit cap where any bar fits it: drawn without it, a
   sparse "o" group whose context held rests and dense bars alone had
   filled its rested bars with Johnny C. Bad's ten-hit bars.
4. The tempo drawn again under the token's class (round 54) is terminal
   at the class: without the floor the class's votes had their
   Witten-Bell share and every class's tempos the rest, and 8 of 46
   written songs sat in another class than their token's, their kits and
   figures drawn under it. At the word's rounding edge (85 counted under
   slow from 83, 130 under medium from 128) the tempo moves inside the
   class, `rhythm.within_band`.

Pack4 refit (98 tables, backup pack.json.r75), the shipped vocabulary
regenerated. Tests: the token's note, the plan's keeping it, kit_note_of
by sample class, the kit table's reach and the limbs' draw, the redraw's
class.

**Measured (sc-tools/kit_density.py: one written song for each FFVI kit
token, the real plan+write path, hits a played bar against the token
song's own from the readings; 46 of 47 tokens drawn in 3000 seeds).**
Median absolute error 1.7 hits a bar before, 1.1 after; mean 4.2 to 3.5;
35 of 46 unchanged. Off-class songs 8 to 0. The Phantom Forest 13.8 to
1.3 (the game 1.0 a played bar, 0.1 a bar); Fanatics 3.7 to 16.4 (15.9),
Under Martial Law 8.2 to 12.4 (14.1), The Day After 8.4 to 4.4 (4.0), all
by the class alone. The limbs' path taken by three songs. Still wrong:
The Wedding Waltz-Duel (part 1), a lone snare at "medium varied" in 3/4,
12.0 against the game's 4.1: its limb is the kick-snare-tom group, whose
habit sets no cap (`group_caps` caps one-letter groups), and the KSt table
at slow in 4/4 holds no snare-only bar under six hits. Dancing Mad (part
2), Grand Finale (part 2), Blackjack and Wild West write a third of their
game's hits (the readings' figure merges what the game strikes
together; untouched here).

**The reader, checked (the owner's third step; no change made).** The role
reader's second clause, a voice on a sample the namer did not call an
instrument, at most two pitches a sample, eight notes or more, labels 15
FFVI voices drums. Fourteen are strikes: Johnny C. Bad's and The Phantom
Train's snare rolls, Wild West's toms, Strago's two-note ostinato (0.21
beats a note), the 336-frame tick in Train, Quiet Beach and The Phantom
Train, "??"'s bell. One is not: The Phantom Forest's lead, held a median
11.9 beats a note, where the 109 drum voices on percussion samples hold a
median 0.45 beats, three quarters under 0.95, none over 7.9. A clause
"and its notes shorter than any drum's" flips that one voice in FFVI and
no other; its token then has no kit, which is the game's truth. Phase 1's
